, then there is some
\Pi_{4}
such that
\Pi_{2}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{4}
and
\Pi_{3}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi_{4}
.

Combining confluence with Proposition
1

is enough to prove soundness beginning with any number of system steps.

Theorem 4

(Soundness)
.

If
\,\vdash C:\tau
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi
,
\operatorname{LN}({C})\subseteq\mathfrak{L}\neq\varnothing
,
and all local programs that
C
executes terminate, then for some
\Pi^{\prime}
and
C^{\prime}
,
\Pi\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
,
C\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{c}}C^{\prime}
, and
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C^{\prime}\right\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C^{\prime}\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}\preceq\Pi^{\prime}
.

Having demonstrated the correspondence between the choreographic and system semantics, we can now prove deadlock-freedom.
Although bisimulation only holds when all executed local programs terminate, deadlock-freedom holds for all projected systems, even in the presence of non-terminating local computations.
By combining the soundness of the type system (Theorems
1
and
2
) and the completeness of EPP (Theorem
3
), we prove that the system either enters an infinite loop for some location or terminates in a value for all locations, which is sufficient.

Theorem 5

(Deadlock Freedom by Design)
.

If
\,\vdash C:\tau
,
\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{L}}}\mathrel{\Longrightarrow^{*}_{S}}\Pi
, and
\operatorname{LN}({C})\subseteq\mathfrak{L}
,
then either every location in
\Pi
maps to a value, or there is some
\Pi^{\prime}
such that
\Pi\mathrel{\Longrightarrow_{S}}\Pi^{\prime}
.

7.
Rocq Development

We now discuss some details of our Rocq formalization and how they differ from the presentation above.
For clarity, we presented
\lambda
qc
in a standard style with named variables in the paper,
but for ease of development, the Rocq code uses a nameless style with de Bruijn indices for both program and type variables.
As in Pirouette
(
Hirsch and Garg 2022
)
, the local language must also use de Bruijn indices, and provide common guarantees about substitution.

This change also forces a different handling of location- and location-set–variable substitution in the Rocq code.
As described in Section
4.2.1
, care must be taken when performing location substitution to avoid variable capture between the namespaces of different locations.
The Rocq encoding, however, avoids this issue by not separating namespaces by location.
Instead, it treats all local variables uniformly as part of a single global namespace of de Bruijn indices.
With this formulation, no capture is possible upon substituting location variables when using the standard capture-avoiding definition of location substitution.

Finally, our formalization proves deadlock freedom in two pieces.
The first uses bisimulation (Theorems
3
and
4
together) to prove deadlock-freedom when all local programs that execute terminate.
The second assumes the presence of a non-terminating local computation and proves deadlock freedom directly.
The proof of Theorem
5
, which does not condition on local (non-)termination
is immediate by combining these, but assumes the law of the excluded middle (LEM) to differentiate between the cases.
We make the LEM a premise to this theorem to explicitly indicate the use of a non-constructive assumption.

8.
Related Work

As mentioned in Section
1
, choreographic programming has seen substantial recent development.
This paper fits into the emerging paradigm of
functional
choreographic programming
with process polymorphism, originally proposed by
Graversen et al. 2024
.
Choreographic programming as a whole arises from concurrency theory and the study of
\pi
-calculi.
We discuss each of these in turn.

8.1.
Functional Choreographic Programming

Choreographic programming was crystallized as an independent paradigm in 2013 by the work of
Carbone and Montesi 2013
, especially Montesi’s Ph.D. thesis
(
Montesi 2013
)
.
For the next 10 years, it advanced, but remained tied to a lower-order model of computing
(
Carbone et al. 2014
;
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2018
;
Cruz-Filipe and Montesi 2017a
;
Lanese et al. 2013
;
Cruz-Filipe and Montesi 2017b
, see, e.g.,)
.

That bind was broken in 2022 by two independent developments: Pirouette
(
Hirsch and Garg 2022
)
and Chor
\lambda

(
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2022
)
.
These works combine the primitives of choreographic programming with
\lambda
calculi, allowing for (sequential) composition of choreographies and program reuse.
However, neither support any type of polymorphism or multiply-located values.

\lambda
qc
is based on Pirouette, inheriting many of its features including
a separate language of messages (the local language)
and out-of-order semantics that require all participants to synchronize on choreographic function applications.
Chor
\lambda
, by contrast, combines the languages of choreographies and messages.
It originally had a strictly sequential semantics
(
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2022
)
,
but recent work showed how to give it an out-of-order semantics
without
global synchronization
(
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2023
)
.
These semantics use commuting conversions, a set of semantics-preserving rewrite rules
such as
(\lambda X\ldotp C_{1})~C_{2}~C_{3}\Rrightarrow(\lambda X\ldotp C_{1}~C_{3})~C_{2}
, and allow reductions inside function bodies.
However, commuting conversions are fragile.
For instance, they fail to preserve types in the presence of named recursive functions, like those in
\lambda
qc
.
That is, the hypothetical rewrite
(\mathop{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}})~C_{2}~C_{3}\Rrightarrow(\mathop{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}~C_{3}})~C_{2}
changes the type of
F
, which is bound in
C_{1}
.
Polymorphism and recursive types produce similar difficulties.

We thus adopt the more restrictive semantics of Pirouette,
and leave finding an appropriate semantics without global synchronization as future work.
Importantly, nothing in this paper relies on this global synchronization except for the
statement
of EPP correctness (Section
6.4
).
We believe an appropriate statement of EPP correctness could support a semantics without this synchronization,
but identifying such a statement requires significant research in its own right.

8.2.
Process Polymorphism

More recently,
Graversen et al. 2024
extended Chor
\lambda
with process polymorphism,
allowing choreographies to abstract over (the identity of) their participant processes.
This extension, PolyChor
\lambda
, introduced the process abstraction and shows how to project it by adding the “AmI” construct to the target language.
PolyChor
\lambda
is based on System
F_{\omega}
, and includes quantification over processes, types, and types of higher-order kinds, but does not include quantification over
sets
of processes.
Their work also does not attempt to define an out-of-order semantics, and instead settles for a sequential, call-by-value semantics for its choreographies.

Bates et al. 2025
separately introduced the idea of
census polymorphism
, which allows choreographies to abstract over the quantity and identity of their participants.
Their work presents extensive real-world uses cases for multiply-located values and abstraction over sets of processes
and highlights several practical implementations (e.g., in MultiChor, ChoRus, and ChoreoTS).
However, the formal model is limited and lacks deadlock-freedom guarantees in the presence of polymorphism.

Our work not only supports first-class location set polymorphism and multiply-located values (missing from PolyChor
\lambda
)
and a deadlock freedom guarantee (missing from the work of
Bates et al. 2025
),
but we mechanically verify all results in Rocq.
To our knowledge, the only prior mechanizations of choreographic results concern Pirouette
(
Hirsch and Garg 2022
)

and simpler lower-order systems
(
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2021b
;
Cruz-Filipe et al. 2021a
)
,
meaning this work contains the first formalization of choreographic process polymorphism or of multiply-located values,
let alone
\lambda
qc
’s full suite of features.

8.3.
Higher-Order Communication

Another important aspect of concurrent systems is
higher-order communication
:
communicating a channel (or channel name) over a channel.
This feature is critical to model systems with a dynamic communication topology, and can be expressed natively in many untyped process calculi such as the
\pi
-calculus and its higher-order variant the
\text{HO}\pi
-calculus
(
Sangiorgi 1993
)
.
Previous work proving deadlock-freedom for concurrent languages with higher-order communication
has relied on advanced typing regimes, like higher-order session types
(
Mostrous and Yoshida 2007
;
Costa et al. 2022
;
Poças et al. 2023
, see, e.g.,)
,
that are highly complex and impose significant restrictions on communication patterns, like requiring them to be from a regular or context-free grammar.

PolyChor
\lambda
supports a restricted form of higher-order communication using
delegation
, where one process can request that another perform an interaction on their behalf.
In particular, they allow processes to communicate entire choreographies, which may themselves contain communication.
However, the type system prevents delegated computation from containing unresolved location variables, limiting the expressive power.

Computing and sending first-class location names is a distinct form of higher-order communication
from both
\pi
-calculus channels and PolyChor
\lambda
’s delegation.
Channels represent a limited capacity to perform a specific action, like sending one value,
while choreographic process names are identifiers of participants who may be asked, by name, to perform many actions.
Meanwhile, providing first-class data and location names to choreographic functions and type abstractions
replicate much of the functionality of delegation, but must be specified manually.

9.
Conclusion

This work presented the choreographic Quick Change calculus,
\lambda
qc
, a choreographic calculus supporting process (set) and type polymorphism, algebraic and recursive data types, multiply-located values, and first-class location names.
While prior choreographic languages implement some of the first three features, none support all three,
and
\lambda
qc
is the first to support first-class location names in a typed choreography.

We showed how to integrate polymorphism over types, processes, and sets of processes using a System F-like type system.
The
\lambda
qc
type system simultaneously allows first-class treatment of (sets of) location names and considers locations type-level values.
Yet it still avoids dependency by restricting the types of computations which use these locations to not depend on values.
To prevent deadlocks,
\lambda
qc
statically ensures that each location knows if it has been selected by any dynamic location computation.

We also showed how to project the constructs in
\lambda
qc
to a network language
that faithfully models the concurrent execution of a multi-party system,
and we proved the classic choreographic result:
all systems projected from well-typed choreographies are deadlock-free.
We mechanically verified proofs of this result as well as others in the paper,
providing the first mechanized formalization for any choreography supporting any form of process polymorphism or multiply-located values.

While there is still a wealth of future research in expanding and refining the features of our calculus, even now, it is able to facilitate practical and safe programming of concurrent systems.
Our paradigmatic example of a distributed thread pool with a load balancer
can be concisely written in
\lambda
qc
, yet is far beyond the capabilities of
prior typed choreographic languages.

A.6.
Location Set Relations

Here we define precisely the containment
\ell\in\rho
, disjointness
\rho_{1}\cap\rho_{2}=\varnothing
, and subset
\rho_{1}\subseteq\rho_{2}
relations, with special care given to how they are defined when the types in question are non-ground.
In particular, we define two versions of containment:
necessary
containment
\square(\ell\in\rho)
, and
possible
containment
\lozenge(\ell\in\rho)
.
The un-annotated containment relation is treated as necessary containment (
\ell\in\rho=\square(\ell\in\rho)
), and the disjointness relation (to be interpreted as necessary disjointness) is defined as follows:

(\rho_{1}\cap\rho_{2}=\varnothing)=\forall\ell\ldotp\neg(\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{1})\wedge\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{2})).

The difference between the two versions of containment is that location sets which are variables (or a singleton of a variable) can possibly contain any location, while variable location sets do not necessarily contain any location.
Note here that the metavariable
\ell
stands for either a type variable
\alpha
or a concrete location
L\in\mathcal{L}
, and the metavariable
\rho
stands for any location set, including possibly a type variable.

\frac{~}{\square(\ell\in\{\ell\})}\frac{\square(\ell\in\rho_{1})}{\square(\ell\in\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2})}\frac{\square(\ell\in\rho_{2})}{\square(\ell\in\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2})}\\
\frac{~}{\lozenge(\ell\in\alpha)}\frac{~}{\lozenge(\ell\in\{\alpha\})}\frac{~}{\lozenge(L\in\{L\})}\frac{\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{1})}{\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2})}\frac{\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{2})}{\lozenge(\ell\in\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2})}

To define the (necessarily a) subset relation, we first note that we cannot use the naïve definition in terms of the necessary containment relation.
That is,
\forall\ell\ldotp\square(\ell\in\rho_{1})\Rightarrow\square(\ell\in\rho_{2})
would not serve as a correct definition for
\rho_{1}\subseteq\rho_{2}
in the presence of type variables.
This is because
\neg\square(\ell\in\alpha)
for every location
\ell
, so with this definition we would have that
\alpha\subseteq\rho
for every set
\rho
.
The subset relation (and relations of
\square
modality in general) should be preserved under substitution, but our example shows that this is not the case when using the naïve definition.
Instead, the subset relation must be defined inductively as follows.

\frac{~}{\varnothing\subseteq\rho}\frac{~}{\rho\subseteq\rho}\frac{\rho\subseteq\rho_{1}}{\rho\subseteq\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}}\frac{\rho\subseteq\rho_{2}}{\rho\subseteq\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}}\frac{\square(\ell\in\rho)}{\{\ell\}\subseteq\rho}\frac{\rho_{1}\subseteq\rho\quad\rho_{2}\subseteq\rho}{\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}\subseteq\rho}

A.7.
Choreography Operational Semantics

\frac{C_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{R\mkern Appendix B
Static Semantics

B.1.
\lambda
qc
Kinding System

\frac{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa\in\Gamma}{\Gamma\vdash\alpha::\kappa}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Var]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\Vdash t_{e}}{\Gamma\vdash t_{e}::*_{e}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Local]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash t_{e}::*_{e}\quad\Gamma\vdash\rho::*_{s}}{\Gamma\vdash t_{e}@\rho::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-At]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}::*\quad\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}::*}{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\to\tau_{2}::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Arrow]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma,\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa\vdash\tau::*}{\Gamma\vdash\forall\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa.\tau::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-All]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}::*\quad\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}::*}{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}\times\tau_{2}::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Prod]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}::*\quad\Gamma\vdash\tau_{2}::*}{\Gamma\vdash\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Sum]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma,\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu*\vdash\tau::*}{\Gamma\vdash\mu\alpha.\tau::*}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Rec]},vcenter}\frac{L\in\mathcal{L}}{\Gamma\vdash L::*_{\ell}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Loc]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\ell::*_{\ell}}{\Gamma\vdash\{\ell\}::*_{s}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Sng]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\rho_{1}::*_{s}\quad\Gamma\vdash\rho_{2}::*_{s}}{\Gamma\vdash\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}::*_{s}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[K-Union]},vcenter}\frac{~}{\Gamma\vdash\cdot}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[WF-EmpCtx]},vcenter}\frac{X\notin\Delta\quad\Gamma\vdash\Delta\quad\quad\Gamma\vdash\tau::*}{\Gamma\vdash\Delta,X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[WF-AddCtx]},vcenter}\frac{x\notin\Sigma\quad\Gamma\vdash\Sigma\quad\quad\Gamma\vdash t_{e}::*_{e}\quad\Gamma\vdash\rho::*_{s}}{\Gamma\vdash\Sigma,\rho.x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[WF-AddLocalCtx]},vcenter}

2.0mu*_{e}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}in}}~{C_{2}}:\tau}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[T-LetLocalTy]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C:t_{e}@\rho_{1}\quad\quad\ell\in\rho_{1}\quad\Gamma\vdash\rho_{2}::*_{s}}{\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\raisebox{0.6458pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.43057pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.21529pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}}\rho_{2}:t_{e}@(\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2})}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[T-Send]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma\vdash\ell::*_{\ell}\quad\Gamma\vdash\rho::*_{s}\quad\quad\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C:\tau}{\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash{\ell}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}[}{d}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}]}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1};}}C:\tau}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[T-Sync]},vcenter}\frac{\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C:\mathsf{bool}@\rho\quad\quad\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C_{1}:\tau\quad\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash C_{2}:\tau}{\Gamma;\Delta;\Sigma\vdash\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}if}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}then}}~{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}else}}~{C_{2}}:\tau}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[T-If]},vcenter}

Appendix C
Network Language

C.1.
Network Language Expressions

\begin{array}[]{@{}llcl@{}}\textrm{Network Program}&E&\Coloneqq&X\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0muE_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathop{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0muE_{1}~E_{2}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\Lambda}{\alpha}\ldotp{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0muE~t\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&(E_{1},E_{2})\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{E}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}case}}~{E}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}of}}~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{E_{1}})~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{E_{2}})\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{E}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}send}}~{E}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}to}}~{\rho}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}recv}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}from}}~{\ell}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{E_{2}}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{E_{2}}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}if}}~{E}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choose}}~{d}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}for}}~{\ell}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}{E}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}L}}\Rightarrow{{E_{1}}_{\bot}})~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}R}}\Rightarrow{{E_{2}}_{\bot}})\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}AmI}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\in}}~{\rho}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}\par\\
\textrm{Network Values}&V&\Coloneqq&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathop{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\Lambda}{\alpha}\ldotp{E}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&(V_{1},V_{2})\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{V}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{V}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{V}\end{array}

C.2.
Transition Labels and Evaluation Contexts

\begin{array}[]{@{}llcl@{}}\textrm{Transition Labels}&l&\Coloneqq&\iota\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\iota_{\text{sync}}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu{L}.{m}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}\\
\\
\textrm{Evaluation Contexts}&\eta&\Coloneqq&[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]~E\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0muV~[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]~t\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&([\boldsymbol{\cdot}],E)\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu(V,[\boldsymbol{\cdot}])\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}case}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}of}}~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{E_{1}})~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{E_{2}})\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}send}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}to}}~{\rho}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{x}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{E}\mkern 3.0mu\mid\mkern 3.0mu\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}in}}~{E}\\
&&\mkern 3.0mu\mid&\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}if}}~{[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}\end{array}

C.3.
Network Language Operational Semantics

\frac{L\triangleright E_{1}\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}}{L\triangleright\eta[E_{1}]\mathrel{{\overset{l\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e_{2}})}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-Ret]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({V})}{L\triangleright V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern f~V\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\iota_{\text{sync}}}}{}}\mathchoice{E{\left[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V\right]}}{E[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V]}{E[F\mapsto f,\mkern 2.0muX\mapsto V]}{E[F\mapsto t]}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-TApp]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({V})}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{V})}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\iota_{\text{sync}}}}{}}V}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-UnfoldFold]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({V_{1}})\quad\operatorname{Val}({V_{2}})}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{(V_{1},V_{2})}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\iota_{\text{sync}}}}{}}V_{1}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-FstPair]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({V_{1}})\quad\operatorname{Val}({V_{2}})}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{(V_{1},V_{2})}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\iota_{\text{sync}}}}{}}V_{2}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-SndPair]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({V})}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}case}}~{(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{V})}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}of}}~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{E_{1}})~(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{E_{2}})\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{\iota_{\text{sync}}}}{}}\mathchoice{E_{1}{\left[X\mapsto V\right]}}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V]}{E_{1}[X\mapsto V\right]}}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto V]}{E_{2}[X\mapsto 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}\mathchoice{C{\left[x\mapsto v\right]}}{C[x\mapsto v]}{C[x\mapsto v]}{C[x\mapsto v]}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-Let]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.75,0.16}\lceil}t{\color[rgb]{0,0.75,0.16}\rfloor}})}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}let}}~{\alpha\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}\mathchoice{E{\left[\alpha\mapsto t\right]}}{E[\alpha\mapsto t]}{E[\alpha\mapsto t]}{E[\alpha\mapsto t]}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-TyLet]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({v})\quad\operatorname{fv}({\rho})=\varnothing}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}send}}~{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}to}}~{\rho}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{v}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho\setminus\{L\}}}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-Send]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{Val}({v})\quad L^{\prime}\neq L}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}recv}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}from}}~{L^{\prime}}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{L^{\prime}}.{v}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}}}{}}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({v})}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-Recv]},vcenter}\frac{\operatorname{fv}({\rho})=\varnothing}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choose}}~{d}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}for}}~{\rho}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}{E}\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{d}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho\setminus\{L\}}}}{}}E}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-Choose]},vcenter}\frac{L^{\prime}\neq L}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}L}}\Rightarrow{E_{1}})~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}R}}\Rightarrow{{E_{2}}_{\bot}})\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{L^{\prime}}.{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}L}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}}}{}}E_{1}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-AllowL]},vcenter}\frac{L^{\prime}\neq L}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}allow}}~{L^{\prime}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}choice}}~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}L}}\Rightarrow{{E_{1}}_{\bot}})~(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}R}}\Rightarrow{E_{2}})\mathrel{{\xRightarrow{{L^{\prime}}.{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.67,0.33,0.1}R}}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\rightsquigarrow}}}}{}}E_{2}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-AllowR]},vcenter}\frac{L\in\rho}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}AmI}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\in}}~{\rho}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{1}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-IAmIn]},vcenter}\frac{L\notin\rho}{L\triangleright\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}AmI}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\in}}~{\rho}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}\mathrel{{\overset{\iota\mkern 4.0mu}{\Longrightarrow}}{}}E_{2}}\quad\textsc{lab={\footnotesize[N-IAmNotIn]},vcenter}

Appendix D
Compilation

D.1.
Network Program Merging

We show the patterns for which
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}
is defined; if there is no matching pattern, then
E_{1}\sqcup E_{2}
is undefined.

\displaystyle\text{undefined}\sqcup\text{undefined}

\displaystyle=\text{undefined}

\displaystyle\text{undefined}\sqcup E_{2}

\displaystyle=E_{2}

\displaystyle E_{1}\sqcup\text{undefined}

\displaystyle=E_{1}

\displaystyle X\sqcup X

\displaystyle=X

\displaystyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}\sqcup\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}()}}

\displaystyle\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})\sqcup\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}ret}}({e})

\displaystyle(E_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2})\sqcup(E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2})

\displaystyle=E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}

\displaystyle(\mathop{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathop{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fun}}}{F}({X})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\coloneqq}}{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(E_{1,1}~E_{1,2})\sqcup(E_{2,1}~E_{2,2})

\displaystyle=(E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1})~(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2})

\displaystyle({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\Lambda}{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\ldotp{E_{1}})\sqcup({\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\Lambda}{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\ldotp{E_{2}})

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\Lambda}{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\ldotp{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(E_{1}~t)\sqcup(E_{2}~t)

\displaystyle=(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})~t

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fold}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}unfold}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(E_{1,1},E_{1,2})\sqcup(E_{2,1},E_{2,2})

\displaystyle=((E_{1,1}\sqcup E_{2,1}),(E_{1,2}\sqcup E_{2,2}))

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}fst}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}snd}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{E_{1}})\sqcup(\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{E_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{(E_{1}\sqcup E_{2})}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}case}}~{E_{1,1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}of}}\\
\mid\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{E_{1,2}}\\
\mid\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{E_{1,3}}\end{array}\right)\sqcup\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}case}}~{E_{2,1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}of}}\\
\mid\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{E_{2,2}}\\
\mid\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}inr}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{E_{2,3}}\end{array}\right)

D.2.
Endpoint Projection

\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}AmI}}~{\ell}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}
is shorthand for
\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}AmI}}\mathord{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}\in}}~{\{\ell\}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}then}}~{E_{1}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}else}}~{E_{2}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}send}}~{\mathchoice{\left\llbracket C\right\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}{\llbracket C\rrbracket_{L}}}~\mathtt{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13}to}}~{\rho}&\text{if}~L=\ell\\

D.3.
Locations Named by a Type or Choreography

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\alpha})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({L})

\displaystyle=\{L\}

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\{\ell\}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\ell})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\rho_{1}\cup\rho_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\rho_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({\rho_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({X})

\displaystyle=\varnothing

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\rho.e})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\rho})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathop{\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}fun}}}{F}({X\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mu\tau})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}~C_{2}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\Lambda}{\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\ldotp{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({C~t})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})\cup\operatorname{LN}({t})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}fold}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}unfold}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({(C_{1},C_{2})})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}fst}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}snd}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}inl}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}inr}}~{C}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}case}}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}of}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}inl}}~{X}\Rightarrow{C_{1}}\\
\mid\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}inl}}~{Y}\Rightarrow{C_{2}}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({C})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}let}}~{\rho.x\mkern 2.0mu{:}\mkern 2.0mut_{e}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}let}}~{\rho.\alpha\mkern 2.0mu{::}\mkern 2.0mu\kappa}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\coloneqq}}{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}in}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({C\mathrel{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\raisebox{0.58122pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-2.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.38751pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\mkern 3.0mu\raisebox{0.19376pt}{$\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\{}\ell{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\}}$}\mkern-1.0mu{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}}\rho})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\ell})\cup\operatorname{LN}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({{\ell}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}[}{d}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}]}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}\rightsquigarrow}}{\rho}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1};}}C})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\ell})\cup\operatorname{LN}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C})

\displaystyle\operatorname{LN}({\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}if}}_{\rho}~{C}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}then}}~{C_{1}}~\mathsf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,1}else}}~{C_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{LN}({\rho})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{1}})\cup\operatorname{LN}({C_{2}})

D.4.
The Less-Than Relation

\frac{~}{\text{undefined}\preceq E}\frac{E_{1}\preceq E_{2}\quad\operatorname{Val}({V})}{E_{1}\preceq V\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2}}\frac{~}{X\preceq E_{2,1}\quad E_{1,2}\preceq E_{2,2}}{E_{1,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{1,2}\preceq E_{2,1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{1,0.23,0.13};}}E_{2,2}}\frac{E_{1}\preceq
